#### # MASTER PROMPT   
####   
#### ## Complete Staff Management, Mission Planning, Geographic Search and Travel Estimation SaaS MVP  
####   
#### ## 1. ROLE AND OBJECTIVE  
####   
#### Act as a senior full-stack software engineer, software architect, database engineer, geospatial engineer, UI/UX designer and QA engineer.  
####   
#### Your task is to build a complete, functional and professionally designed SaaS application directly inside the current terminal workspace.  
####   
#### The application must allow staffing managers to manage their workforce, maintain staff location records, check availability, find the most suitable staff for a mission, calculate the distance between a staff member's registered location and the mission location, and estimate travel time according to the selected mode of transport.  
####   
#### This is an implementation task. Do not merely produce a plan, a mockup, pseudocode or another prompt.  
####   
#### You must implement the actual application, database, authentication, business logic, pages, forms, integrations, validation, tests and documentation.  
####   
#### The final product must be a usable MVP with real database persistence and real functionality.  
####   
#### ## 2. CORE BUSINESS PROBLEM  
####   
#### A manager may manage hundreds or thousands of staff members distributed across different cities, regions and countries.  
####   
#### When a new mission is received, the manager needs to know quickly:  
####   
#### - Which staff members are available?  
#### - Which staff members have the required skills?  
#### - Which staff members are closest to the mission location?  
#### - What is the distance between each candidate's registered location and the mission?  
#### - What is the estimated journey time by car, public transport or another supported mode?  
#### - Which candidates can reasonably reach the mission location?  
#### - How can the manager contact suitable staff immediately?  
#### - Which staff members have missing or outdated location information?  
#### - How can the manager manage all these operations without manually inspecting hundreds of records?  
####   
#### The application's primary objective is to help the manager find and evaluate suitable staff for a mission in seconds.  
####   
#### ## 3. MANDATORY DEVELOPMENT RULES  
####   
#### Before writing code:  
####   
#### 1. Inspect the current working directory and existing project.  
#### 2. Identify the framework, package manager, dependencies, existing components, environment variables and database configuration.  
#### 3. Preserve valid existing code wherever practical.  
#### 4. Check the official documentation for the installed versions of all unfamiliar libraries and APIs.  
#### 5. Identify which integrations are configured and which require credentials.  
#### 6. Create an implementation checklist.  
#### 7. Start implementing the application directly in the workspace.  
####   
#### Do not ask questions when sensible technical defaults are sufficient.  
####   
#### Do not replace the application with static mockups.  
####   
#### Do not fabricate data, coordinates, routes, travel times, staff availability or successful API responses.  
####   
#### Do not create buttons that do nothing.  
####   
#### Do not expose private API keys or privileged database credentials in client-side code.  
####   
#### Do not disable TypeScript, linting or tests to conceal errors.  
####   
#### Do not claim that a feature works unless it has been implemented and appropriately verified.  
####   
#### Work in coherent phases, run checks frequently, diagnose errors and correct them before proceeding.  
####   
#### If a service cannot be tested because credentials are missing, implement the integration, document the configuration requirement and distinguish unverified behavior from verified behavior.  
####   
#### ## 4. TECHNOLOGY STACK  
####   
#### Use the following stack where compatible with the existing project:  
####   
#### - Next.js App Router.  
#### - React.  
#### - TypeScript with strict mode.  
#### - Tailwind CSS.  
#### - shadcn/ui.  
#### - Lucide React.  
#### - Supabase Authentication.  
#### - Supabase PostgreSQL.  
#### - Supabase Row Level Security.  
#### - Zod.  
#### - React Hook Form.  
#### - Mapbox GL JS.  
#### - A suitable route and travel-time provider, such as Mapbox Directions or another compatible routing API.  
#### - A geocoding provider suitable for the selected country.  
#### - Papa Parse or another maintained CSV parser.  
#### - Vitest and React Testing Library where compatible.  
#### - Playwright for end-to-end tests where the environment permits.  
####   
#### Use server components by default and client components where interactivity requires them.  
####   
#### Keep geocoding and routing providers behind server-side service interfaces so providers can be changed without rewriting the application.  
####   
#### Do not assume that postal codes, units, road networks or transport options are identical in every country.  
####   
#### ## 5. PRODUCT NAME AND CONFIGURATION  
####   
#### Working product name: StaffFlow.  
####   
#### Keep the product name, default preferences and feature configuration in centralized configuration files.  
####   
#### The application must not be permanently tied to one country.  
####   
#### Support country-specific:  
####   
#### - Postal-code validation.  
#### - Location resolution.  
#### - Phone-number formatting.  
#### - Distance units where appropriate.  
#### - Currency and locale settings if needed in future.  
#### - Routing and travel-mode availability.  
####   
#### The initial setup must be configurable for the launch market, without hardcoding the application architecture to a single country.  
####   
#### ## 6. USER ROLES AND MVP SCOPE  
####   
#### ### Manager  
####   
#### The manager can:  
####   
#### - Register and log in.  
#### - Create and manage staff records.  
#### - Import and export staff.  
#### - Update staff availability.  
#### - Maintain registered location information.  
#### - Create and manage missions.  
#### - Search for suitable staff near a mission.  
#### - Compare distances and estimated travel times.  
#### - View staff and mission locations on a map.  
#### - Contact staff.  
#### - Review activity history and notifications.  
#### - Manage account and search preferences.  
####   
#### ### Staff member  
####   
#### For the MVP, staff members are records created or imported by a manager.  
####   
#### Staff members do not have their own accounts, login pages or self-registration.  
####   
#### ### Client  
####   
#### Clients do not have accounts or access to staff tracking in the MVP.  
####   
#### Do not build a client portal or expose internal staff locations to clients.  
####   
#### ### Future architecture  
####   
#### Keep the data model extensible for future staff accounts, client accounts, recruitment, job listings, organizational memberships and additional countries.  
####   
#### Do not implement these future modules now.  
####   
#### ## 7. LOCATION TRACKING  IMPORTANT SCOPE LIMITATION  
####   
#### The MVP must implement **registered-location management, not real-time GPS tracking**.  
####   
#### The manager can consult the latest location information recorded for a staff member, based on the postal code and coordinates stored in that person's profile.  
####   
#### The application must distinguish between:  
####   
#### 1. **Registered location:** the location entered or maintained in the staff record.  
#### 2. **Last location verification:** the last time the postal code was successfully resolved or verified.  
#### 3. **Mission location:** the location associated with a staffing assignment.  
#### 4. **Live location:** not implemented in the MVP.  
####   
#### Do not implement:  
####   
#### - Continuous GPS tracking.  
#### - Background location collection.  
#### - Live staff movement on the map.  
#### - Automatic collection of staff device coordinates.  
#### - A live location feed for clients.  
#### - Claims that a staff member is physically present at a location.  
####   
#### Never imply that a registered postal code proves a person's current physical position.  
####   
#### Display clear labels such as:  
####   
#### - Registered Location.  
#### - Location Verified.  
#### - Location Needs Review.  
#### - Last Verified.  
####   
#### If a staff member's registered location changes, update the record only through a valid manager action or a properly authorized future integration.  
####   
#### Location timestamps must describe the actual operation performed. Do not use a recent timestamp to make an old location appear current.  
####   
#### ## 8. APPLICATION ROUTES  
####   
#### Public routes:  
####   
#### - `/`  landing page.  
#### - `/login`  login.  
#### - `/signup`  manager registration.  
#### - `/auth/callback`  authentication callback if required.  
#### - `/auth/verify`  verification flow where applicable.  
#### - `/forgot-password`  password recovery if password authentication is enabled.  
####   
#### Protected routes:  
####   
#### - `/dashboard`  operational dashboard.  
#### - `/staff`  staff directory.  
#### - `/staff/new`  create staff.  
#### - `/staff/[id]`  staff profile.  
#### - `/staff/[id]/edit`  edit staff.  
#### - `/staff/import`  CSV import.  
#### - `/find-staff`  geographic staff search.  
#### - `/missions`  mission directory.  
#### - `/missions/new`  create mission.  
#### - `/missions/[id]`  mission details.  
#### - `/missions/[id]/edit`  edit mission.  
#### - `/missions/[id]/staff`  find, compare and select staff for a mission.  
#### - `/activity`  activity history.  
#### - `/notifications`  notifications.  
#### - `/settings`  general settings.  
#### - `/settings/profile`  manager profile.  
#### - `/settings/security`  account security.  
#### - `/settings/location`  geographic search and travel preferences.  
####   
#### You may combine related screens where that improves usability, but all required capabilities must remain accessible.  
####   
#### Protect every private route and enforce authorization on the server.  
####   
#### ## 9. DESIGN SYSTEM  
####   
#### Create a premium, professional B2B SaaS interface.  
####   
#### Suggested palette:  
####   
#### - Background: `#F7F8FA`.  
#### - Surface: `#FFFFFF`.  
#### - Main text: `#172033`.  
#### - Secondary text: `#667085`.  
#### - Primary action: `#3157D5`.  
#### - Primary hover: `#2647B8`.  
#### - Border: `#E5E7EB`.  
####   
#### Use Inter or a similarly readable font.  
####   
#### Use:  
####   
#### - Consistent spacing.  
#### - Clear hierarchy.  
#### - Compact data tables.  
#### - Accessible status indicators.  
#### - Responsive cards.  
#### - Clear form labels.  
#### - Subtle shadows.  
#### - Consistent button sizes.  
#### - Skeleton loading states.  
#### - Empty states.  
#### - Useful error messages.  
#### - Confirmation dialogs for destructive actions.  
#### - Toasts for completed operations.  
####   
#### Avoid excessive gradients, unnecessary animations and decorative elements that interfere with operational work.  
####   
#### Respect keyboard accessibility and `prefers-reduced-motion`.  
####   
#### ### Global navigation  
####   
#### Sidebar:  
####   
#### 1. Dashboard.  
#### 2. Staff Directory.  
#### 3. Find Staff.  
#### 4. Missions.  
#### 5. Import Staff.  
#### 6. Activity.  
#### 7. Notifications.  
#### 8. Settings.  
####   
#### Include:  
####   
#### - Product logo and name.  
#### - Current manager profile.  
#### - Account menu.  
#### - Logout.  
#### - Active navigation state.  
#### - Mobile navigation.  
####   
#### Every button must perform its advertised action, show a pending state when needed and report errors appropriately.  
####   
#### ## 10. AUTHENTICATION  
####   
#### Use Supabase Authentication.  
####   
#### Implement:  
####   
#### - Manager signup.  
#### - Login.  
#### - Email verification or OTP according to the configured authentication method.  
#### - Session persistence.  
#### - Protected routes.  
#### - Logout.  
#### - Password recovery if password authentication is enabled.  
####   
#### Signup fields:  
####   
#### - Full name.  
#### - Email.  
#### - Password when applicable.  
#### - Confirm password when applicable.  
#### - Company name, optional.  
#### - Country or primary operating region.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Create Account.  
#### - Sign In.  
#### - Show/Hide Password.  
#### - Forgot Password.  
#### - Verify Email or Verify Code, when applicable.  
#### - Resend Code, when applicable.  
####   
#### Validate all fields.  
####   
#### Handle expired sessions, incorrect credentials, duplicate accounts, expired verification codes and rate limits.  
####   
#### Do not create unauthorized manager profiles or trust a client-supplied owner ID.  
####   
#### ## 11. DATABASE SCHEMA  
####   
#### Create version-controlled SQL migrations, constraints, indexes and RLS policies.  
####   
#### ### `profiles`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID matching the authenticated user.  
#### - `full_name`.  
#### - `company_name`.  
#### - `country_code`.  
#### - `timezone`.  
#### - `default_search_radius_km`.  
#### - `default_transport_mode`.  
#### - `created_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
####   
#### ### `staff`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID.  
#### - `owner_id`: UUID.  
#### - `staff_code`: optional.  
#### - `full_name`: required.  
#### - `phone`: optional.  
#### - `email`: optional.  
#### - `country_code`: required.  
#### - `postal_code`: required where geographic search is used.  
#### - `postal_code_normalized`.  
#### - `city`.  
#### - `region`.  
#### - `latitude`: nullable.  
#### - `longitude`: nullable.  
#### - `location_status`: `pending`, `resolved`, or `failed`.  
#### - `location_updated_at`: nullable.  
#### - `availability_status`: `available`, `unavailable`, or `unknown`.  
#### - `skills`.  
#### - `notes`.  
#### - `last_contacted_at`: nullable.  
#### - `created_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
#### - `archived_at`: nullable.  
####   
#### A missing availability value must never be treated as available.  
####   
#### A record with unresolved coordinates must not appear in geographic search results as if its location were verified.  
####   
#### ### `missions`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID.  
#### - `owner_id`: UUID.  
#### - `mission_reference`: optional unique identifier within the manager's account.  
#### - `title`: required.  
#### - `description`: optional.  
#### - `country_code`: required.  
#### - `postal_code`: required where available.  
#### - `city`: optional.  
#### - `region`: optional.  
#### - `latitude`: nullable until resolved.  
#### - `longitude`: nullable until resolved.  
#### - `location_status`: `pending`, `resolved`, or `failed`.  
#### - `location_updated_at`: nullable.  
#### - `required_skills`: array or normalized relation.  
#### - `required_staff_count`: integer greater than zero.  
#### - `scheduled_start_at`: nullable.  
#### - `scheduled_end_at`: nullable.  
#### - `transport_mode`: configured default for this mission.  
#### - `status`: `draft`, `open`, `staffing_in_progress`, `staffed`, `completed`, or `cancelled`.  
#### - `notes`: optional.  
#### - `created_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
#### - `archived_at`: nullable.  
####   
#### ### `mission_staff`  
####   
#### This table records the manager's staff selections for a mission.  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID.  
#### - `owner_id`: UUID.  
#### - `mission_id`: UUID.  
#### - `staff_id`: UUID.  
#### - `assignment_status`: `selected`, `contacted`, `confirmed`, `declined`, `cancelled`, or `completed`.  
#### - `selected_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
#### - `notes`: optional.  
####   
#### Prevent duplicate active assignments for the same staff member and mission where appropriate.  
####   
#### A staff selection is not proof that the person accepted or attended the mission.  
####   
#### ### `activity_logs`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`.  
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - `staff_id`: nullable.  
#### - `mission_id`: nullable.  
#### - `event_type`.  
#### - `description`.  
#### - `metadata`: JSONB with a data-minimization policy.  
#### - `created_at`.  
####   
#### Log relevant events:  
####   
#### - Staff created or updated.  
#### - Availability changed.  
#### - Location resolved or failed.  
#### - Mission created or updated.  
#### - Staff selected for a mission.  
#### - Staff contacted.  
#### - Assignment status changed.  
#### - CSV import or export completed.  
#### - Routing request failed where operationally useful.  
####   
#### Never store passwords, API secrets or unnecessary personal information in logs.  
####   
#### ### `notifications`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`.  
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - `type`.  
#### - `title`.  
#### - `message`.  
#### - `read_at`: nullable.  
#### - `created_at`.  
####   
#### Create appropriate notifications for imports, location failures and mission-related operations.  
####   
#### ### Optional route calculation cache  
####   
#### If appropriate, create a private `route_estimates` table or equivalent cache.  
####   
#### Potential fields:  
####   
#### - `id`.  
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - `origin_latitude`.  
#### - `origin_longitude`.  
#### - `destination_latitude`.  
#### - `destination_longitude`.  
#### - `transport_mode`.  
#### - `provider`.  
#### - `distance_meters`.  
#### - `duration_seconds`.  
#### - `estimated_at`.  
#### - `expires_at`.  
####   
#### Only cache valid successful responses.  
####   
#### Avoid storing unnecessary personal details in cache keys.  
####   
#### Cache access must be scoped securely. Use a deterministic cache key based on normalized coordinates and transport mode when appropriate.  
####   
#### Do not persist estimates indefinitely. Configure an expiry policy and refresh stale results when needed.  
####   
#### ### Database indexes  
####   
#### Add suitable indexes for:  
####   
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - Staff availability.  
#### - Normalized postal code.  
#### - Country and city.  
#### - Staff location coordinates or spatial indexes where supported.  
#### - Mission status and scheduled time.  
#### - Mission location.  
#### - Mission-to-staff relationships.  
#### - Activity timestamps.  
#### - Route cache lookup and expiry.  
####   
#### ## 12. SECURITY AND ROW LEVEL SECURITY  
####   
#### Enable RLS on all private tables.  
####   
#### Ensure managers can access only their own:  
####   
#### - Profiles.  
#### - Staff.  
#### - Missions.  
#### - Mission assignments.  
#### - Activity logs.  
#### - Notifications.  
#### - Private route estimates.  
####   
#### Validate ownership for both the mission and the staff member when creating a mission assignment.  
####   
#### Do not trust IDs submitted by the browser without checking ownership.  
####   
#### Never expose the Supabase service-role key.  
####   
#### Never expose private staff data through public API endpoints.  
####   
#### Use least privilege, input validation and server-side authorization.  
####   
#### Protect against IDOR, injection, XSS, unsafe exports and excessively large requests.  
####   
#### Do not allow a manager to calculate a route between records they are not authorized to access.  
####

#### ## 15. CREATE AND EDIT STAFF  
####   
#### Routes:  
####   
#### - `/staff/new`  
#### - `/staff/[id]/edit`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - Full Name.  
#### - Staff Code.  
#### - Phone.  
#### - Email.  
#### - Country.  
#### - Postal Code.  
#### - City.  
#### - Region.  
#### - Skills.  
#### - Availability.  
#### - Notes.  
####   
#### Location buttons:  
####   
#### - Validate Location.  
#### - Revalidate Location.  
#### - Clear Location.  
####   
#### Form buttons:  
####   
#### - Save Staff.  
#### - Save and Add Another.  
#### - Save Changes.  
#### - Cancel.  
####   
#### When a postal code is entered or changed:  
####   
#### 1. Normalize it.  
#### 2. Validate it for the selected country where possible.  
#### 3. Resolve the location using the configured provider.  
#### 4. Store valid returned coordinates.  
#### 5. Show the resolved location.  
#### 6. Record success or failure.  
#### 7. Never invent coordinates.  
####   
#### A record may be saved without coordinates only if the business rules permit it. Mark it unresolved and exclude it from geographic results until resolved.  
####   
#### Prevent duplicate submissions and preserve form data after recoverable errors.  
####   
#### Warn before discarding unsaved changes.  
####

####   
#### Display:  
####   
#### - Full name.  
#### - Staff code.  
#### - Contact information.  
#### - Registered location.  
#### - Availability.  
#### - Skills.  
#### - Notes.  
#### - Location verification status.  
#### - Last location verification.

#### - Relevant activity history.

#### - Find Missions Nearby, if implemented as a real action.  
#### - Archive Staff.  
#### - Back to Directory.  
####

####   
#### Do not claim a call was completed or a message was sent.  
####   
#### Display the registered location on a map if coordinates are available.  
####   
#### Clearly label it as the registered location, not a live GPS position.  
####   
#### ## 17. LOCATION MANAGEMENT  
####   
#### Implement a dedicated server-side location service.  
####   
#### It must support:  
####   
#### - Country-specific postal-code normalization.  
#### - Geocoding.  
#### - Coordinate validation.  
#### - Location status management.  
#### - Caching.  
#### - Bounded concurrency for bulk resolution.  
#### - Provider-specific error handling.  
#### - Rate-limit handling.  
####   
#### When a location changes:  
####   
#### 1. Mark the previous result stale or pending.  
#### 2. Resolve the new location.  
#### 3. Validate the result.  
#### 4. Update coordinates only on success.  
#### 5. Update the verification timestamp.  
#### 6. Record the event.  
#### 7. Report failure clearly.  
####   
#### Allow the manager to:  
####   
#### - View unresolved staff locations.  
#### - Resolve pending locations.  
#### - Retry failed locations.  
#### - Revalidate a selected record.  
#### - Review location failures.  
####   
#### Never repeatedly geocode an unchanged postal code without a reason.  
####   
#### Respect provider terms and quotas.  
####   
#### ## 18. MISSIONS DIRECTORY  `/missions`  
####   
#### Build a complete mission management page.  
####   
#### ### Header  
####   
#### Title: `Missions`  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - `New Mission`.  
#### - `Refresh`.  
####   
#### ### Search and filters  
####   
#### Allow filtering by:  
####   
#### - Mission title.  
#### - Mission reference.  
#### - Postal code.  
#### - City.  
#### - Country.  
#### - Status.  
#### - Scheduled date.  
#### - Staffing progress.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Search.  
#### - Clear Filters.  
#### - Export, if implemented.  
####   
#### ### Mission list  
####   
#### Display:  
####   
#### - Mission reference.  
#### - Title.  
#### - Location.  
#### - Scheduled start.  
#### - Required staff.  
#### - Selected staff.  
#### - Status.  
#### - Updated date.  
#### - Actions.  
####   
#### Row actions:  
####   
#### - View Mission.  
#### - Edit Mission.  
#### - Find Staff.  
#### - View Selected Staff.  
#### - Cancel Mission, with confirmation.  
####   
#### Implement pagination and real database queries.  
####   
#### ### Empty state  
####   
#### Display a clear message and a `Create Mission` button.  
####   
#### ## 19. CREATE MISSION  `/missions/new`  
####   
#### Create a mission form with the following fields.  
####   
#### ### Mission details  
####   
#### - Mission Title  required.  
#### - Mission Reference  optional.  
#### - Description  optional.  
#### - Required Staff Count  required, integer greater than zero.  
#### - Required Skills  optional multi-select.  
#### - Scheduled Start  optional.  
#### - Scheduled End  optional.  
#### - Notes  optional.  
####   
#### Validate that the end time is not earlier than the start time.  
####   
#### ### Mission location  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - Country.  
#### - Postal Code.  
#### - City.  
#### - Region.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Validate Location.  
#### - Clear Location.  
####   
#### Resolve coordinates using the configured geocoding provider.  
####   
#### Do not permit distance-based search until the mission has valid coordinates.  
####   
#### ### Travel preferences  
####   
#### Field:  
####   
#### - Preferred Transport Mode.  
####   
#### Supported modes depend on the configured routing provider. Potential modes:  
####   
#### - Driving.  
#### - Walking.  
#### - Cycling.  
#### - Public Transport, only if a real provider supports it.  
####   
#### If public transport is unsupported, disable the option and explain why. Never simulate public transport routes using car-routing data.  
####   
#### ### Mission status  
####   
#### New missions begin as `draft` or `open`, according to the selected workflow.  
####   
#### ### Form buttons  
####   
#### - Save Draft.  
#### - Create Mission.  
#### - Cancel.  
####   
#### On successful creation:  
####   
#### - Persist the mission.  
#### - Record an activity event.  
#### - Redirect to the mission detail page.  
#### - Offer `Find Staff for This Mission`.  
####   
#### ## 20. MISSION DETAILS  `/missions/[id]`  
####   
#### Display:  
####   
#### - Mission title and reference.  
#### - Description.  
#### - Location.  
#### - Map.  
#### - Scheduled time.  
#### - Required staff count.  
#### - Selected staff count.  
#### - Required skills.  
#### - Preferred transport mode.  
#### - Status.  
#### - Notes.  
#### - Activity history.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Edit Mission.  
#### - Find Staff.  
#### - View Selected Staff.  
#### - Change Status.  
#### - Cancel Mission.  
#### - Back to Missions.  
####   
#### Display a clear staffing indicator, for example, `3 of 5 staff selected`, based on real records.  
####   
#### Do not mark a mission fully staffed merely because the required number of people were selected if the business rules require confirmed assignments.  
####   
#### Distinguish selected, contacted, confirmed, declined and completed assignments.  
####   
#### ## 21. FIND STAFF FOR A MISSION  `/missions/[id]/staff`  
####   
#### This page is central to the product.  
####   
#### The manager must be able to evaluate potential staff members for a specific mission.  
####   
#### ### Search header  
####   
#### Display:  
####   
#### - Mission title.  
#### - Mission location.  
#### - Required staff count.  
#### - Currently selected staff count.  
#### - Mission status.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Back to Mission.  
#### - Edit Mission.  
#### - Refresh Results.  
####   
#### ### Filters  
####   
#### - Search radius: 5, 10, 20, 30 or 50 km.  
#### - Availability: Available Only, All Statuses.  
#### - Required Skills.  
#### - Transport Mode.  
#### - Maximum results, optional.  
####   
#### Default radius: 10 km.  
####   
#### Default availability filter: Available Only.  
####   
#### Never include `unknown` availability as available.  
####   
#### ### Search behavior  
####   
#### When the mission has valid coordinates:  
####   
#### 1. Query only staff belonging to the authenticated manager.  
#### 2. Exclude archived staff.  
#### 3. Exclude staff with missing or invalid coordinates.  
#### 4. Apply availability filters.  
#### 5. Apply skill filters.  
#### 6. Calculate geographic distance for candidate filtering.  
#### 7. Retrieve route estimates where supported.  
#### 8. Rank candidates using the configured ranking strategy.  
#### 9. Display results with clear distance and duration labels.  
#### 10. Allow the manager to select staff for the mission.  
####   
#### Do not require route calculation for every staff member before producing any results. Use a staged approach:  
####   
#### - First identify nearby candidates using geographic distance.  
#### - Then retrieve route estimates for a bounded set of candidates.  
#### - Show route calculation progress and partial results where appropriate.  
#### - Handle route provider limits gracefully.  
####   
#### Do not silently represent straight-line distance as road distance.  
####   
#### ### Candidate result cards or table  
####   
#### Display:  
####   
#### - Staff name.  
#### - Availability.  
#### - Skills.  
#### - Registered postal code.  
#### - City or region.  
#### - Straight-line distance.  
#### - Road distance, if available.  
#### - Estimated travel time.  
#### - Transport mode.  
#### - Route estimate status.  
#### - Location verification status.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - View Profile.  
#### - Call.  
#### - WhatsApp.  
#### - Compare.  
#### - Select for Mission.  
####   
#### If a route estimate is unavailable, display `Travel time unavailable` rather than inventing one.  
####   
#### ### Selection  
####   
#### When the manager selects a candidate:  
####   
#### - Verify ownership of both records.  
#### - Prevent duplicate assignments.  
#### - Persist the assignment.  
#### - Record an activity event.  
#### - Update mission staffing counts.  
#### - Update the UI after server confirmation.  
####   
#### Selection is not confirmation of attendance or acceptance.  
####   
#### Provide a way to:  
####   
#### - Remove a selected candidate.  
#### - Mark a candidate as contacted.  
#### - Mark an assignment as confirmed.  
#### - Mark an assignment as declined.  
#### - View all selected staff.  
####   
#### Use confirmation dialogs where appropriate.  
####   
#### ## 22. DISTANCE CALCULATION  
####   
#### Implement a dedicated, tested geospatial service.  
####   
#### For every candidate, distinguish these measurements.  
####   
#### ### A. Straight-line distance  
####   
#### Calculate the geographic distance between:  
####   
#### - The staff member's registered coordinates.  
#### - The mission's coordinates.  
####   
#### Use the Haversine formula or an appropriate geographic database function.  
####   
#### Return a distance in meters and format it for display.  
####   
#### Example display:  
####   
#### - `2.4 km straight-line distance`.  
####   
#### Use this measurement for initial candidate filtering where appropriate.  
####   
#### Never label straight-line distance as road distance.  
####   
#### ### B. Road distance  
####   
#### When a routing provider is configured and supports the selected transport mode, request a real route estimate.  
####   
#### Return:  
####   
#### - Route distance in meters.  
#### - Estimated duration in seconds.  
#### - Provider identifier.  
#### - Calculation timestamp.  
#### - Transport mode.  
#### - Estimate status.  
####   
#### Display a clear label:  
####   
#### - `Road distance`.  
#### - `Estimated travel time`.  
####   
#### Use the provider's returned route distance, not the Haversine result.  
####   
#### Do not calculate road distance by multiplying straight-line distance by an arbitrary factor.  
####   
#### ### C. Distance formatting  
####   
#### Format values sensibly:  
####   
#### - Meters for very short distances.  
#### - Kilometers for longer distances.  
#### - Consistent decimal precision.  
#### - No false precision.  
####   
#### If coordinates are missing or invalid, return an explicit unavailable result.  
####   
#### ### D. Geographic accuracy  
####   
#### Postal-code coordinates are approximate. Explain that distance and route estimates depend on the precision of the registered locations.  
####   
#### A route from a postal-code centroid is not necessarily the route from the person's exact home address.  
####   
#### Do not display exact-looking accuracy that the source data cannot justify.  
####   
#### ## 23. TRAVEL TIME ESTIMATION BY TRANSPORT MODE  
####   
#### The application must support transport-specific estimates where a reliable provider exists.  
####   
#### ### Driving  
####   
#### Use a real routing API configured for driving.  
####   
#### Display:  
####   
#### - Road distance.  
#### - Estimated driving time.  
#### - Provider and calculation status.  
#### - Time of last calculation.  
####   
#### Use live traffic-aware duration only if the provider supports it, the required option is configured and the response genuinely includes traffic-aware information.  
####   
#### Otherwise label the result `Estimated driving time` and do not claim that current traffic is included.  
####   
#### ### Walking  
####   
#### Use a walking route profile only when supported by the provider.  
####   
#### Do not estimate walking time using a driving route duration.  
####   
#### ### Cycling  
####   
#### Use a cycling route profile only when supported.  
####   
#### Do not show a cycling estimate when the provider cannot calculate cycling routes.  
####   
#### ### Public transport  
####   
#### Public transport often depends on schedules, routes and service availability.  
####   
#### Use a real public transport routing provider if configured.  
####   
#### If no such provider is configured:  
####   
#### - Disable or mark Public Transport as unavailable.  
#### - Explain that public transport estimates are not currently supported.  
#### - Do not reuse car travel times.  
#### - Do not present an arbitrary bus or train speed as a real route estimate.  
####   
#### ### Optional fallback using average speed  
####   
#### A configurable average-speed model may be used as a fallback only when the routing service cannot provide an estimate and the manager explicitly permits fallback estimates.  
####   
#### The fallback must:  
####   
#### - Be configurable by transport mode.  
#### - Be clearly labelled as an approximate fallback.  
#### - Never be presented as a real routed journey.  
#### - Never imply current traffic or transit schedules.  
#### - Be omitted when a reliable fallback cannot be justified.  
####   
#### Use road distance for driving fallback only if an actual road distance is available.  
####   
#### If only straight-line distance exists, label any calculation based on it as a rough approximation and do not treat it as an operationally reliable arrival estimate.  
####   
#### Do not silently substitute a fallback estimate for a real route result.  
####   
#### ### Transport selector  
####   
#### Provide a transport selector on the mission staff search page.  
####   
#### Changing the transport mode must:  
####   
#### 1. Preserve the mission and search filters.  
#### 2. Retrieve a new estimate using the selected mode.  
#### 3. Update candidate travel times.  
#### 4. Update the ranking when travel time is part of the ranking strategy.  
#### 5. Display an unsupported-mode message if necessary.  
#### 6. Handle API errors without losing the staff search results.  
####   
#### ### Estimate caching  
####   
#### Cache successful route estimates where appropriate.  
####   
#### Cache keys must include:  
####   
#### - Origin coordinates.  
#### - Destination coordinates.  
#### - Transport mode.  
#### - Relevant routing options.  
#### - Provider configuration where needed.  
####   
#### Set an appropriate expiry time.  
####   
#### Never reuse a driving estimate for walking, cycling or public transport.  
####   
#### Refresh estimates when locations change or when cached results expire.  
####   
#### Avoid excessive API calls by calculating routes only for relevant candidates and using bounded concurrency.  
####   
#### ## 24. CANDIDATE RANKING  
####   
#### The manager must be able to sort results by:  
####   
#### - Straight-line distance.  
#### - Road distance, where available.  
#### - Estimated travel time, where available.  
#### - Name.  
####   
#### Use the selected sorting method explicitly.  
####   
#### The default ranking should prioritize:  
####   
#### 1. Required availability.  
#### 2. Required skills.  
#### 3. Inclusion within the selected geographic radius.  
#### 4. Estimated travel time when a valid route estimate exists.  
#### 5. Distance as a secondary criterion.  
####   
#### Do not create a misleading single suitability score unless its formula is defined, implemented and explained.  
####   
#### If some candidates have route estimates and others do not, label the missing estimates clearly and do not silently treat missing values as zero.  
####   
#### Allow the manager to sort by distance independently from estimated travel time.  
####   
#### ## 25. MAP AND LIST SYNCHRONIZATION  
####   
#### Use Mapbox GL JS.  
####   
#### For the mission staff search page, display:  
####   
#### - Mission location.  
#### - Staff registered locations.  
#### - Selected staff markers.  
#### - Matching staff markers.  
#### - Marker clusters when needed.  
#### - Search radius.  
#### - Map controls.  
#### - Legend.  
#### - Result count.  
####   
#### Interactions:  
####   
#### - Clicking a marker highlights its staff card.  
#### - Clicking a card highlights its marker.  
#### - Selecting a candidate updates the selection state.  
#### - Clicking the mission marker displays mission details.  
#### - Cluster clicks zoom into the cluster.  
#### - Changing the radius refreshes the relevant search results.  
#### - Changing the transport mode refreshes route estimates, not staff coordinates.  
####   
#### When the provider supports route geometry, optionally display the selected candidate's route to the mission.  
####   
#### Display route lines only when actual route geometry was returned.  
####   
#### Do not draw fabricated routes between points.  
####   
#### If route geometry is unavailable, display distance and duration without a route line.  
####   
#### Keep the map responsive.  
####   
#### If Mapbox is not configured, preserve the directory, mission search and distance features that can function without the map, and show an informative configuration message.  
####   
#### ## 26. GENERAL FIND STAFF PAGE  `/find-staff`  
####   
#### Provide geographic staff search independent of a saved mission.  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - Country.  
#### - Search postal code or supported location.  
#### - Search radius.  
#### - Availability.  
#### - Required skills.  
#### - Transport mode where a destination and routing estimate are available.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Find Staff.  
#### - Clear Search.  
#### - List View.  
#### - Map View.  
#### - Export Results.  
#### - View Profile.  
#### - Call.  
#### - WhatsApp.  
####   
#### Calculate straight-line distance between the search location and staff coordinates.  
####   
#### When a routing destination and supported transport mode are available, calculate road distance and travel time.  
####   
#### If the manager searches only by city or postal code without creating a mission, treat that search point as the destination reference and label the results accordingly.  
####   
#### Never confuse this reference point with the staff member's live location.  
####   
#### ## 27. CSV IMPORT  `/staff/import`  
####   
#### Support `.csv` uploads for large staff lists.  
####   
#### Expected fields:  
####   
#### - `full_name`  
#### - `staff_code`  
#### - `phone`  
#### - `email`  
#### - `country_code`  
#### - `postal_code`  
#### - `city`  
#### - `region`  
#### - `skills`  
#### - `availability`  
#### - `notes`  
####   
#### Implement:  
####   
#### - Downloadable CSV template.  
#### - File selection.  
#### - Header mapping.  
#### - Preview.  
#### - Field validation.  
#### - Duplicate detection.  
#### - Row-level errors.  
#### - Import progress.  
#### - Bounded batches.  
#### - Location resolution.  
#### - Partial failure reporting.  
#### - Downloadable error report.  
#### - Import completion summary.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Download CSV Template.  
#### - Choose File.  
#### - Upload and Preview.  
#### - Confirm Import.  
#### - Download Error Report.  
#### - View Imported Staff.  
#### - Import Another File.  
#### - Cancel.  
####   
#### The application must handle at least 1,000 records in a realistic test environment.  
####   
#### Never trust an `owner_id` supplied by the CSV.  
####   
#### Do not create one unbounded geocoding request for every row.  
####   
#### Resolve unique postal codes in batches where supported.  
####   
#### Do not calculate route estimates for every imported staff member. Route calculations should occur when a manager searches or compares candidates for a specific destination.  
####   
#### ## 28. CSV EXPORT

#### Support exporting:

#### - Selected mission staff.

#### Buttons:

#### - Export All.  
#### - Export Filtered.  
#### - Export Selected.  
#### - Download CSV.  
####   
#### Escape CSV values correctly and protect against spreadsheet formula injection.  
####   
#### For mission candidate exports, clearly identify:  
####   
#### - Straight-line distance.  
#### - Road distance, if available.  
#### - Estimated travel time.  
#### - Transport mode.  
#### - Estimate status.  
#### - Calculation timestamp.  
####   
#### Do not silently truncate records.

#### ## 33. API AND SERVICE ARCHITECTURE  
####   
#### Keep provider integrations isolated in dedicated modules.  
####   
#### Create clean interfaces for:  
####   
#### - Geocoding.  
#### - Route calculation.  
#### - Distance calculation.  
#### - Staff search.  
#### - Mission management.  
#### - CSV processing.  
#### - Activity logging.  
#### - Notifications.  
####   
#### Use server-side service functions or route handlers as appropriate.  
####   
#### All external responses must be validated before being saved or returned to the client.  
####   
#### Handle:  
####   
#### - Network failures.  
#### - Provider rate limits.  
#### - Timeouts.  
#### - Invalid coordinates.  
#### - Unsupported transport modes.  
#### - Missing API keys.  
#### - Empty route results.  
#### - Invalid geocoding results.  
####   
#### Use typed results and explicit error states.  
####   
#### Do not couple the entire UI directly to one routing provider.  
####   
#### Do not expose provider secrets in client-side bundles.  
####   
#### ## 34. PERFORMANCE REQUIREMENTS  
####   
#### Support approximately 1,000 staff records per manager without major usability problems.  
####   
#### Implement:  
####   
#### - Server-side pagination.  
#### - Indexed filtering.  
#### - Efficient database queries.  
#### - Debounced search.  
#### - Bounded batch processing.  
#### - Bounded route API concurrency.  
#### - Route estimate caching.  
#### - Map clustering.  
#### - Efficient dashboard aggregation.  
#### - Avoidance of unnecessary full-table downloads.  
#### - Avoidance of repeated geocoding.  
#### - Avoidance of repeated route calculations.  
####   
#### Do not calculate every possible staff-to-mission route on every page load.  
####   
#### Use a staged search strategy to reduce provider usage and response times.  
####   
#### Do not cache private records across different managers.  
####   
#### Measure performance where possible and report actual findings.  
####   
#### ## 35. PRIVACY AND DATA ACCURACY  
####   
#### Treat staff information as private personal data.  
####   
#### Implement:  
####   
#### - Data minimization.  
#### - Manager-only access.  
#### - Secure database policies.  
#### - Clear location status.  
#### - Clear distinction between registered location and live location.  
#### - Secure exports.  
#### - No public staff profiles.  
#### - No covert location collection.  
#### - No client access to internal staff coordinates.  
#### - Appropriate privacy notice.  
#### - Configurable data-retention considerations.  
####   
#### Postal-code coordinates are approximate. Travel estimates depend on the accuracy of the source locations, the routing provider, route availability and any traffic or transit information included by the provider.  
####   
#### Do not claim a travel estimate is guaranteed.  
####   
#### Do not claim real-time tracking exists.  
####   
#### ## 36. ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES  
####   
#### Create `.env.example` with placeholders for the actual selected configuration.  
####   
#### Potential variables:  
####   
#### - `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`  
#### - `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` or the correct public key name for the installed Supabase SDK.  
#### - `NEXT_PUBLIC_MAPBOX_ACCESS_TOKEN`  
#### - Server-side geocoding API key, if required.  
#### - Server-side routing API key, if required.  
####   
#### Use current SDK conventions.  
####   
#### Do not expose service-role keys or secret routing credentials through `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`.  
####   
#### Update `.gitignore` to exclude actual environment files while retaining `.env.example`.  
####   
#### Document each variable and how to obtain it.  
####   
#### If a service is not configured, do not crash unrelated features or fabricate results.

#### Do not mark tests as passed unless they were actually executed.  
####   
#### Use a test database or suitable mocks, and distinguish mocked provider tests from tests against a real external service.  
####   
#### ## 39. FINAL ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA  
####   
#### The MVP is complete only when the following have been implemented and verified as far as the environment permits.  
####   
#### ### Authentication and security  
####   
#### - Managers can authenticate.  
#### - Private routes are protected.  
#### - RLS isolates manager data.  
#### - Secrets are not exposed.  
#### - Ownership is enforced on the server.  
####   
#### ### Staff management  
####   
#### - Staff can be created, edited, viewed and archived.  
#### - Availability changes persist.  
#### - Records survive page refresh.  
#### - CSV import and export work.  
#### - Large imports report errors accurately.  
####   
#### ### Missions  
####   
#### - Managers can create and edit missions.  
#### - Mission locations can be resolved.  
#### - Required staff counts are validated.  
#### - Staff can be selected for missions.  
#### - Assignment status is stored accurately.  
####   
#### ### Geographic search  
####   
#### - Nearby staff are identified using valid coordinates.  
#### - Radius filtering works.  
#### - Straight-line distance is correctly calculated.  
#### - Road distance is distinguished from straight-line distance.  
#### - Results can be sorted by distance.  
#### - Missing coordinates are handled explicitly.  
####   
#### ### Travel time  
####   
#### - Supported transport modes call the appropriate routing service.  
#### - Driving estimates are not reused for walking, cycling or public transport.  
#### - Unsupported modes are clearly identified.  
#### - Fallback estimates, if implemented, are explicitly labelled.  
#### - Missing route estimates are never fabricated.  
#### - Estimates can be refreshed when necessary.  
####   
#### ### Map  
####   
#### - Mission and registered staff locations are displayed.  
#### - Map markers correspond to real records.  
#### - List and map selection are synchronized.  
#### - Route geometry is displayed only when returned by a real routing provider.  
#### - Missing configuration produces a useful message.  
####   
#### ### Tracking  
####   
#### - The manager can view registered staff locations and verification timestamps.  
#### - The application does not claim to provide live GPS tracking.  
#### - Clients do not have access to staff tracking.  
####   
#### ### Quality  
####   
#### - TypeScript checks pass.  
#### - Lint passes.  
#### - Relevant tests pass.  
#### - Production build succeeds.  
#### - Responsive layouts work.  
#### - No critical button is inert.  
#### - Errors are handled visibly.  
#### - External service limitations are documented.  
####   
#### ## 40. IMPLEMENTATION ORDER  
####   
#### ### Phase 1: Foundation  
####   
#### - Inspect project.  
#### - Configure framework and dependencies.  
#### - Configure Supabase.  
#### - Create schema and migrations.  
#### - Implement authentication and RLS.  
#### - Build shared application layout.  
####   
#### ### Phase 2: Staff management  
####   
#### - Staff CRUD.  
#### - Directory.  
#### - Search and filters.  
#### - Profiles.  
#### - Availability.  
#### - Archiving.  
####   
#### ### Phase 3: Location services  
####   
#### - Geocoding provider.  
#### - Postal-code normalization.  
#### - Coordinate resolution.  
#### - Location status.  
#### - Distance calculation.  
#### - Tests.  
####   
#### ### Phase 4: Missions  
####   
#### - Mission CRUD.  
#### - Mission location resolution.  
#### - Mission status.  
#### - Staff selection.  
#### - Assignment management.  
####   
#### ### Phase 5: Geographic search and travel estimates  
####   
#### - Radius search.  
#### - Straight-line distance.  
#### - Routing provider.  
#### - Transport-mode handling.  
#### - Travel-time estimates.  
#### - Route cache.  
#### - Candidate ranking.  
#### - Map and list synchronization.  
####   
#### ### Phase 6: CSV and operational features  
####   
#### - Import preview.  
#### - Bulk import.  
#### - Error reports.  
#### - Export.  
#### - Dashboard.  
#### - Activity history.  
#### - Notifications.  
#### - Settings.  
####   
#### ### Phase 7: Quality assurance  
####   
#### - Unit tests.  
#### - Integration tests.  
#### - Security tests.  
#### - End-to-end tests where possible.  
#### - Lint.  
#### - Type checks.  
#### - Production build.  
#### - Responsive review.  
#### - Fix detected errors.  
####   
#### Prioritize reliable functionality over decorative design work.  
####   
#### Do not build all the pages as static UI first and leave the backend for later.  
####   
#### Complete critical workflows end to end.  
####   
#### ## 41. TERMINAL WORKFLOW  
####   
#### After every significant phase:  
####   
#### 1. Inspect modified files.  
#### 2. Run relevant checks.  
#### 3. Review actual error output.  
#### 4. Diagnose the cause.  
#### 5. Fix the problem.  
#### 6. Rerun the failing check.  
#### 7. Verify that the fix did not break related functionality.  
####   
#### Do not delete user data or run destructive database operations without authorization.  
####   
#### Do not perform irreversible remote migrations without explaining their impact.  
####   
#### Do not conceal errors by disabling checks.  
####   
#### If external credentials are missing, distinguish configuration limitations from software defects.  
####   
#### Continue working until all feasible implementation and verification steps are complete.  
####   
#### ## 42. FINAL DELIVERY REPORT  
####   
#### At completion, report:  
####   
#### 1. Implemented features.  
#### 2. Main files and database migrations created or modified.  
#### 3. Authentication and database setup status.  
#### 4. Required environment variables.  
#### 5. Geocoding provider and configuration.  
#### 6. Routing provider and supported transport modes.  
#### 7. Whether distance is straight-line, road-based or both.  
#### 8. How travel time is calculated and what limitations apply.  
#### 9. Commands actually executed.  
#### 10. Actual test, lint, type-check and build results.  
#### 11. Features tested against real services.  
#### 12. Features verified only through mocks.  
#### 13. Remaining configuration requirements.  
#### 14. Known limitations.  
#### 15. Exact command to run the application.  
####   
#### Never claim that a service is fully functional if it was not configured or tested.  
####   
#### ## FINAL INSTRUCTION  
####   
#### Start by inspecting the existing terminal workspace and then implement StaffFlow directly.  
####   
#### Do not return another prompt, a tutorial or a list of suggestions instead of the application.  
####   
#### Build the actual code, database migrations, authentication, staff directory, mission management, location services, geographic search, distance calculations, travel-time estimation, interactive map, CSV workflows, dashboard, security policies, tests and documentation.  
####   
#### The central workflow must allow a manager to create a mission, specify its location, find suitable available staff, compare their distance and estimated journey time by supported transport mode, select candidates and manage the mission.  
####   
#### The MVP must provide registered-location visibility, not live GPS tracking.  
####   
#### **Implement, test, correct and verify the application before reporting completion. Clearly distinguish working functionality from anything that still requires configuration.**

#### # MASTER PROMPT   
####   
#### ## Complete Staff Management, Mission Planning, Geographic Search and Travel Estimation SaaS MVP  
####   
#### ## 1. ROLE AND OBJECTIVE  
####   
#### Act as a senior full-stack software engineer, software architect, database engineer, geospatial engineer, UI/UX designer and QA engineer.  
####   
#### Your task is to build a complete, functional and professionally designed SaaS application directly inside the current terminal workspace.  
####   
#### The application must allow staffing managers to manage their workforce, maintain staff location records, check availability, find the most suitable staff for a mission, calculate the distance between a staff member's registered location and the mission location, and estimate travel time according to the selected mode of transport.  
####   
#### This is an implementation task. Do not merely produce a plan, a mockup, pseudocode or another prompt.  
####   
#### You must implement the actual application, database, authentication, business logic, pages, forms, integrations, validation, tests and documentation.  
####   
#### The final product must be a usable MVP with real database persistence and real functionality.  
####   
#### ## 2. CORE BUSINESS PROBLEM  
####   
#### A manager may manage hundreds or thousands of staff members distributed across different cities, regions and countries.  
####   
#### When a new mission is received, the manager needs to know quickly:  
####   
#### - Which staff members are available?  
#### - Which staff members have the required skills?  
#### - Which staff members are closest to the mission location?  
#### - What is the distance between each candidate's registered location and the mission?  
#### - What is the estimated journey time by car, public transport or another supported mode?  
#### - Which candidates can reasonably reach the mission location?  
#### - How can the manager contact suitable staff immediately?  
#### - Which staff members have missing or outdated location information?  
#### - How can the manager manage all these operations without manually inspecting hundreds of records?  
####   
#### The application's primary objective is to help the manager find and evaluate suitable staff for a mission in seconds.  
####   
#### ## 3. MANDATORY DEVELOPMENT RULES  
####   
#### Before writing code:  
####   
#### 1. Inspect the current working directory and existing project.  
#### 2. Identify the framework, package manager, dependencies, existing components, environment variables and database configuration.  
#### 3. Preserve valid existing code wherever practical.  
#### 4. Check the official documentation for the installed versions of all unfamiliar libraries and APIs.  
#### 5. Identify which integrations are configured and which require credentials.  
#### 6. Create an implementation checklist.  
#### 7. Start implementing the application directly in the workspace.  
####   
#### Do not ask questions when sensible technical defaults are sufficient.  
####   
#### Do not replace the application with static mockups.  
####   
#### Do not fabricate data, coordinates, routes, travel times, staff availability or successful API responses.  
####   
#### Do not create buttons that do nothing.  
####   
#### Do not expose private API keys or privileged database credentials in client-side code.  
####   
#### Do not disable TypeScript, linting or tests to conceal errors.  
####   
#### Do not claim that a feature works unless it has been implemented and appropriately verified.  
####   
#### Work in coherent phases, run checks frequently, diagnose errors and correct them before proceeding.  
####   
#### If a service cannot be tested because credentials are missing, implement the integration, document the configuration requirement and distinguish unverified behavior from verified behavior.  
####   
#### ## 4. TECHNOLOGY STACK  
####   
#### Use the following stack where compatible with the existing project:  
####   
#### - Next.js App Router.  
#### - React.  
#### - TypeScript with strict mode.  
#### - Tailwind CSS.  
#### - shadcn/ui.  
#### - Lucide React.  
#### - Supabase Authentication.  
#### - Supabase PostgreSQL.  
#### - Supabase Row Level Security.  
#### - Zod.  
#### - React Hook Form.  
#### - Mapbox GL JS.  
#### - A suitable route and travel-time provider, such as Mapbox Directions or another compatible routing API.  
#### - A geocoding provider suitable for the selected country.  
#### - Papa Parse or another maintained CSV parser.  
#### - Vitest and React Testing Library where compatible.  
#### - Playwright for end-to-end tests where the environment permits.  
####   
#### Use server components by default and client components where interactivity requires them.  
####   
#### Keep geocoding and routing providers behind server-side service interfaces so providers can be changed without rewriting the application.  
####   
#### Do not assume that postal codes, units, road networks or transport options are identical in every country.  
####   
#### ## 5. PRODUCT NAME AND CONFIGURATION  
####   
#### Working product name: StaffFlow.  
####   
#### Keep the product name, default preferences and feature configuration in centralized configuration files.  
####   
#### The application must not be permanently tied to one country.  
####   
#### Support country-specific:  
####   
#### - Postal-code validation.  
#### - Location resolution.  
#### - Phone-number formatting.  
#### - Distance units where appropriate.  
#### - Currency and locale settings if needed in future.  
#### - Routing and travel-mode availability.  
####   
#### The initial setup must be configurable for the launch market, without hardcoding the application architecture to a single country.  
####   
#### ## 6. USER ROLES AND MVP SCOPE  
####   
#### ### Manager  
####   
#### The manager can:  
####   
#### - Register and log in.  
#### - Create and manage staff records.  
#### - Import and export staff.  
#### - Update staff availability.  
#### - Maintain registered location information.  
#### - Create and manage missions.  
#### - Search for suitable staff near a mission.  
#### - Compare distances and estimated travel times.  
#### - View staff and mission locations on a map.  
#### - Contact staff.  
#### - Review activity history and notifications.  
#### - Manage account and search preferences.  
####   
#### ### Staff member  
####   
#### For the MVP, staff members are records created or imported by a manager.  
####   
#### Staff members do not have their own accounts, login pages or self-registration.  
####   
#### ### Client  
####   
#### Clients do not have accounts or access to staff tracking in the MVP.  
####   
#### Do not build a client portal or expose internal staff locations to clients.  
####   
#### ### Future architecture  
####   
#### Keep the data model extensible for future staff accounts, client accounts, recruitment, job listings, organizational memberships and additional countries.  
####   
#### Do not implement these future modules now.  
####   
#### ## 7. LOCATION TRACKING  IMPORTANT SCOPE LIMITATION  
####   
#### The MVP must implement **registered-location management, not real-time GPS tracking**.  
####   
#### The manager can consult the latest location information recorded for a staff member, based on the postal code and coordinates stored in that person's profile.  
####   
#### The application must distinguish between:  
####   
#### 1. **Registered location:** the location entered or maintained in the staff record.  
#### 2. **Last location verification:** the last time the postal code was successfully resolved or verified.  
#### 3. **Mission location:** the location associated with a staffing assignment.  
#### 4. **Live location:** not implemented in the MVP.  
####   
#### Do not implement:  
####   
#### - Continuous GPS tracking.  
#### - Background location collection.  
#### - Live staff movement on the map.  
#### - Automatic collection of staff device coordinates.  
#### - A live location feed for clients.  
#### - Claims that a staff member is physically present at a location.  
####   
#### Never imply that a registered postal code proves a person's current physical position.  
####   
#### Display clear labels such as:  
####   
#### - Registered Location.  
#### - Location Verified.  
#### - Location Needs Review.  
#### - Last Verified.  
####   
#### If a staff member's registered location changes, update the record only through a valid manager action or a properly authorized future integration.  
####   
#### Location timestamps must describe the actual operation performed. Do not use a recent timestamp to make an old location appear current.  
####   
#### ## 8. APPLICATION ROUTES  
####   
#### Public routes:  
####   
#### - `/`  landing page.  
#### - `/login`  login.  
#### - `/signup`  manager registration.  
#### - `/auth/callback`  authentication callback if required.  
#### - `/auth/verify`  verification flow where applicable.  
#### - `/forgot-password`  password recovery if password authentication is enabled.  
####   
#### Protected routes:  
####   
#### - `/dashboard`  operational dashboard.  
#### - `/staff`  staff directory.  
#### - `/staff/new`  create staff.  
#### - `/staff/[id]`  staff profile.  
#### - `/staff/[id]/edit`  edit staff.  
#### - `/staff/import`  CSV import.  
#### - `/find-staff`  geographic staff search.  
#### - `/missions`  mission directory.  
#### - `/missions/new`  create mission.  
#### - `/missions/[id]`  mission details.  
#### - `/missions/[id]/edit`  edit mission.  
#### - `/missions/[id]/staff`  find, compare and select staff for a mission.  
#### - `/activity`  activity history.  
#### - `/notifications`  notifications.  
#### - `/settings`  general settings.  
#### - `/settings/profile`  manager profile.  
#### - `/settings/security`  account security.  
#### - `/settings/location`  geographic search and travel preferences.  
####   
#### You may combine related screens where that improves usability, but all required capabilities must remain accessible.  
####   
#### Protect every private route and enforce authorization on the server.  
####   
#### ## 9. DESIGN SYSTEM  
####   
#### Create a premium, professional B2B SaaS interface.  
####   
#### Suggested palette:  
####   
#### - Background: `#F7F8FA`.  
#### - Surface: `#FFFFFF`.  
#### - Main text: `#172033`.  
#### - Secondary text: `#667085`.  
#### - Primary action: `#3157D5`.  
#### - Primary hover: `#2647B8`.  
#### - Border: `#E5E7EB`.  
####   
#### Use Inter or a similarly readable font.  
####   
#### Use:  
####   
#### - Consistent spacing.  
#### - Clear hierarchy.  
#### - Compact data tables.  
#### - Accessible status indicators.  
#### - Responsive cards.  
#### - Clear form labels.  
#### - Subtle shadows.  
#### - Consistent button sizes.  
#### - Skeleton loading states.  
#### - Empty states.  
#### - Useful error messages.  
#### - Confirmation dialogs for destructive actions.  
#### - Toasts for completed operations.  
####   
#### Avoid excessive gradients, unnecessary animations and decorative elements that interfere with operational work.  
####   
#### Respect keyboard accessibility and `prefers-reduced-motion`.  
####   
#### ### Global navigation  
####   
#### Sidebar:  
####   
#### 1. Dashboard.  
#### 2. Staff Directory.  
#### 3. Find Staff.  
#### 4. Missions.  
#### 5. Import Staff.  
#### 6. Activity.  
#### 7. Notifications.  
#### 8. Settings.  
####   
#### Include:  
####   
#### - Product logo and name.  
#### - Current manager profile.  
#### - Account menu.  
#### - Logout.  
#### - Active navigation state.  
#### - Mobile navigation.  
####   
#### Every button must perform its advertised action, show a pending state when needed and report errors appropriately.  
####   
#### ## 10. AUTHENTICATION  
####   
#### Use Supabase Authentication.  
####   
#### Implement:  
####   
#### - Manager signup.  
#### - Login.  
#### - Email verification or OTP according to the configured authentication method.  
#### - Session persistence.  
#### - Protected routes.  
#### - Logout.  
#### - Password recovery if password authentication is enabled.  
####   
#### Signup fields:  
####   
#### - Full name.  
#### - Email.  
#### - Password when applicable.  
#### - Confirm password when applicable.  
#### - Company name, optional.  
#### - Country or primary operating region.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Create Account.  
#### - Sign In.  
#### - Show/Hide Password.  
#### - Forgot Password.  
#### - Verify Email or Verify Code, when applicable.  
#### - Resend Code, when applicable.  
####   
#### Validate all fields.  
####   
#### Handle expired sessions, incorrect credentials, duplicate accounts, expired verification codes and rate limits.  
####   
#### Do not create unauthorized manager profiles or trust a client-supplied owner ID.  
####   
#### ## 11. DATABASE SCHEMA  
####   
#### Create version-controlled SQL migrations, constraints, indexes and RLS policies.  
####   
#### ### `profiles`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID matching the authenticated user.  
#### - `full_name`.  
#### - `company_name`.  
#### - `country_code`.  
#### - `timezone`.  
#### - `default_search_radius_km`.  
#### - `default_transport_mode`.  
#### - `created_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
####   
#### ### `staff`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID.  
#### - `owner_id`: UUID.  
#### - `staff_code`: optional.  
#### - `full_name`: required.  
#### - `phone`: optional.  
#### - `email`: optional.  
#### - `country_code`: required.  
#### - `postal_code`: required where geographic search is used.  
#### - `postal_code_normalized`.  
#### - `city`.  
#### - `region`.  
#### - `latitude`: nullable.  
#### - `longitude`: nullable.  
#### - `location_status`: `pending`, `resolved`, or `failed`.  
#### - `location_updated_at`: nullable.  
#### - `availability_status`: `available`, `unavailable`, or `unknown`.  
#### - `skills`.  
#### - `notes`.  
#### - `last_contacted_at`: nullable.  
#### - `created_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
#### - `archived_at`: nullable.  
####   
#### A missing availability value must never be treated as available.  
####   
#### A record with unresolved coordinates must not appear in geographic search results as if its location were verified.  
####   
#### ### `missions`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID.  
#### - `owner_id`: UUID.  
#### - `mission_reference`: optional unique identifier within the manager's account.  
#### - `title`: required.  
#### - `description`: optional.  
#### - `country_code`: required.  
#### - `postal_code`: required where available.  
#### - `city`: optional.  
#### - `region`: optional.  
#### - `latitude`: nullable until resolved.  
#### - `longitude`: nullable until resolved.  
#### - `location_status`: `pending`, `resolved`, or `failed`.  
#### - `location_updated_at`: nullable.  
#### - `required_skills`: array or normalized relation.  
#### - `required_staff_count`: integer greater than zero.  
#### - `scheduled_start_at`: nullable.  
#### - `scheduled_end_at`: nullable.  
#### - `transport_mode`: configured default for this mission.  
#### - `status`: `draft`, `open`, `staffing_in_progress`, `staffed`, `completed`, or `cancelled`.  
#### - `notes`: optional.  
#### - `created_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
#### - `archived_at`: nullable.  
####   
#### ### `mission_staff`  
####   
#### This table records the manager's staff selections for a mission.  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`: UUID.  
#### - `owner_id`: UUID.  
#### - `mission_id`: UUID.  
#### - `staff_id`: UUID.  
#### - `assignment_status`: `selected`, `contacted`, `confirmed`, `declined`, `cancelled`, or `completed`.  
#### - `selected_at`.  
#### - `updated_at`.  
#### - `notes`: optional.  
####   
#### Prevent duplicate active assignments for the same staff member and mission where appropriate.  
####   
#### A staff selection is not proof that the person accepted or attended the mission.  
####   
#### ### `activity_logs`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`.  
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - `staff_id`: nullable.  
#### - `mission_id`: nullable.  
#### - `event_type`.  
#### - `description`.  
#### - `metadata`: JSONB with a data-minimization policy.  
#### - `created_at`.  
####   
#### Log relevant events:  
####   
#### - Staff created or updated.  
#### - Availability changed.  
#### - Location resolved or failed.  
#### - Mission created or updated.  
#### - Staff selected for a mission.  
#### - Staff contacted.  
#### - Assignment status changed.  
#### - CSV import or export completed.  
#### - Routing request failed where operationally useful.  
####   
#### Never store passwords, API secrets or unnecessary personal information in logs.  
####   
#### ### `notifications`  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - `id`.  
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - `type`.  
#### - `title`.  
#### - `message`.  
#### - `read_at`: nullable.  
#### - `created_at`.  
####   
#### Create appropriate notifications for imports, location failures and mission-related operations.  
####   
#### ### Optional route calculation cache  
####   
#### If appropriate, create a private `route_estimates` table or equivalent cache.  
####   
#### Potential fields:  
####   
#### - `id`.  
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - `origin_latitude`.  
#### - `origin_longitude`.  
#### - `destination_latitude`.  
#### - `destination_longitude`.  
#### - `transport_mode`.  
#### - `provider`.  
#### - `distance_meters`.  
#### - `duration_seconds`.  
#### - `estimated_at`.  
#### - `expires_at`.  
####   
#### Only cache valid successful responses.  
####   
#### Avoid storing unnecessary personal details in cache keys.  
####   
#### Cache access must be scoped securely. Use a deterministic cache key based on normalized coordinates and transport mode when appropriate.  
####   
#### Do not persist estimates indefinitely. Configure an expiry policy and refresh stale results when needed.  
####   
#### ### Database indexes  
####   
#### Add suitable indexes for:  
####   
#### - `owner_id`.  
#### - Staff availability.  
#### - Normalized postal code.  
#### - Country and city.  
#### - Staff location coordinates or spatial indexes where supported.  
#### - Mission status and scheduled time.  
#### - Mission location.  
#### - Mission-to-staff relationships.  
#### - Activity timestamps.  
#### - Route cache lookup and expiry.  
####   
#### ## 12. SECURITY AND ROW LEVEL SECURITY  
####   
#### Enable RLS on all private tables.  
####   
#### Ensure managers can access only their own:  
####   
#### - Profiles.  
#### - Staff.  
#### - Missions.  
#### - Mission assignments.  
#### - Activity logs.  
#### - Notifications.  
#### - Private route estimates.  
####   
#### Validate ownership for both the mission and the staff member when creating a mission assignment.  
####   
#### Do not trust IDs submitted by the browser without checking ownership.  
####   
#### Never expose the Supabase service-role key.  
####   
#### Never expose private staff data through public API endpoints.  
####   
#### Use least privilege, input validation and server-side authorization.  
####   
#### Protect against IDOR, injection, XSS, unsafe exports and excessively large requests.  
####   
#### Do not allow a manager to calculate a route between records they are not authorized to access.  
####

#### ## 15. CREATE AND EDIT STAFF  
####   
#### Routes:

#### - `/staff/[id]/edit`

#### - Clear Location.

#### Form buttons:

#### - Save Staff.  
#### - Save and Add Another.  
#### - Save Changes.  
#### - Cancel.  
####   
#### When a postal code is entered or changed:  
####   
#### 1. Normalize it.  
#### 2. Validate it for the selected country where possible.  
#### 3. Resolve the location using the configured provider.  
#### 4. Store valid returned coordinates.  
#### 5. Show the resolved location.  
#### 6. Record success or failure.  
#### 7. Never invent coordinates.  
####   
#### A record may be saved without coordinates only if the business rules permit it. Mark it unresolved and exclude it from geographic results until resolved.  
####   
#### Prevent duplicate submissions and preserve form data after recoverable errors.  
####   
#### Warn before discarding unsaved changes.  
####

#### - Archive Staff.  
#### - Back to Directory.

####

####

#### ## 17. LOCATION MANAGEMENT  
####   
#### Implement a dedicated server-side location service.  
####   
#### It must support:  
####   
#### - Country-specific postal-code normalization.  
#### - Geocoding.  
#### - Coordinate validation.  
#### - Location status management.  
#### - Caching.  
#### - Bounded concurrency for bulk resolution.  
#### - Provider-specific error handling.  
#### - Rate-limit handling.  
####   
#### When a location changes:  
####   
#### 1. Mark the previous result stale or pending.  
#### 2. Resolve the new location.  
#### 3. Validate the result.  
#### 4. Update coordinates only on success.  
#### 5. Update the verification timestamp.  
#### 6. Record the event.  
#### 7. Report failure clearly.  
####   
#### Allow the manager to:  
####   
#### - View unresolved staff locations.  
#### - Resolve pending locations.  
#### - Retry failed locations.  
#### - Revalidate a selected record.  
#### - Review location failures.  
####   
#### Never repeatedly geocode an unchanged postal code without a reason.  
####   
#### Respect provider terms and quotas.  
####

#### ## 19. CREATE MISSION  `/missions/new`  
####   
#### Create a mission form with the following fields.  
####   
#### ### Mission details  
####   
#### - Mission Title  required.  
#### - Mission Reference  optional.  
#### - Description  optional.  
#### - Required Staff Count  required, integer greater than zero.  
#### - Required Skills  optional multi-select.  
#### - Scheduled Start  optional.  
#### - Scheduled End  optional.  
#### - Notes  optional.  
####   
#### Validate that the end time is not earlier than the start time.  
####   
#### ### Mission location  
####   
#### Fields:  
####   
#### - Country.  
#### - Postal Code.  
#### - City.  
#### - Region.  
####   
#### Buttons:  
####   
#### - Validate Location.  
#### - Clear Location.  
####   
#### Resolve coordinates using the configured geocoding provider.  
####   
#### Do not permit distance-based search until the mission has valid coordinates.  
####   
#### ### Travel preferences  
####   
#### Field:  
####   
#### - Preferred Transport Mode.  
####   
#### Supported modes depend on the configured routing provider. Potential modes:  
####   
#### - Driving.  
#### - Walking.  
#### - Cycling.  
#### - Public Transport, only if a real provider supports it.  
####   
#### If public transport is unsupported, disable the option and explain why. Never simulate public transport routes using car-routing data.  
####   
#### ### Mission status  
####   
#### New missions begin as `draft` or `open`, according to the selected workflow.  
####   
#### ### Form buttons  
####   
#### - Save Draft.  
#### - Create Mission.  
#### - Cancel.  
####   
#### On successful creation:  
####   
#### - Persist the mission.  
#### - Record an activity event.  
#### - Redirect to the mission detail page.  
#### - Offer `Find Staff for This Mission`.

#### - Cancel Mission.  
#### - Back to Missions.
